in writing. in order to have a functioning european market for goods, it is important that the interests of consumers are also taken into account. this is not the case with the amendments made by the committee on internal market affairs to the commission's draft law laying down rules on nominal quantities of pre-packaged goods. the rapporteur has proposed rules on packaging for, among other things, butter, milk, pasta and rice. i want european consumers to have a choice of many products and there is no intrinsic value in regulating away today's swedish milk cartons of, say, 300 ml. today, consumers can decide for themselves what they want to buy by looking at price comparisons. we will legislate to improve the conditions of competition and to increase consumer protection. on the other hand, we will clear away unnecessary rules, in line with the commission's ambition. i therefore voted for a narrower but more restrictive eu, free of unnecessary regulation which imposes unnecessary costs on the food industry.
in writing. the report deals with a proposal for deregulation, but at the same time harmonization, of the size of food packaging. on the one hand, from the point of view of the internal market, it may be good to have standards which ultimately benefit the consumer. on the other hand, the report proposes far-reaching detailed regulation at eu level. the amendments voted by parliament's committees mention that studies carried out by the commission show that comparative prices are generally neither used nor understood by consumers. these statements are to some extent disrespectful to the citizens of the member states and thus demonstrate an unpleasant attitude towards the population. i voted against the report today because it involves regulation of an area which should really be a matter for each member state to decide and should not be at eu level.
in writing. once again, a report on the eu's common foreign and security policy, the cfsp, has been put on the agenda. it is the junist party's firm belief that foreign and security policy is a matter that must be dealt with at the level of the member states, not the eu. the report also states that the european parliament must be consulted when guidelines for the cfsp are drawn up for each new year. parliament is thus trying to usurp more power, something junilistan opposes. for the reasons mentioned above, i voted against the report in today's vote.
in writing. we voted for the report. we interpret the concept of 'minimum income' in paragraph 4 (f) as implying a guaranteed decent standard of living, as we are in favour of a guaranteed decent standard of living but against the introduction of state-relegated minimum wages.
in writing. the moderate delegation has today decided to abstain in a vote on the current situation in combating violence against women and possible future measures. we have not been able to support the report because it seeks to make national competences the subject of eu decisions. we are firmly convinced that gender equality and criminal policy issues should be decided by the member states and that they are best placed to do so. the report also calls for the establishment of a european union-wide network of national authorities responsible for the protection of the environment. we believe that the state must fulfill its primary task, to protect citizens from criminal attacks, regardless of the victim and the sex of the perpetrator. the emphasis should be on the responsibility of the individual perpetrator of violence rather than on a societal view that reduces the responsibility of the individual.
in writing. i very much share the rapporteur's views on this subject. it is of the utmost importance that opinion is formed on these issues in the member states. i voted in favour of the report because it does not explicitly advocate legislation at eu level. a number of recommendations are made to both the commission and the member states to combat violence against women. i believe that this is ultimately a matter for the national parliaments to take any legislative action that may be necessary.
in writing. the moderate delegation has today chosen to abstain in a vote on equality between women and men in the eu. we moderates want to fight the injustice of inequality and prejudice. however, it is not the eu's job to define what action should be taken in the member states or by other actors in society. we cannot therefore support the report, which makes a number of proposals which fall within the competence of the member states, for example in the provision of childcare, where the cultural characteristics and traditions of the member states are so different. the report also deals with areas which even the member states should not regulate, such as whether political parties are considering strategies to bring more women into their party structure. however, there are other important aspects which are addressed in the report, such as the need to collect comparable statistics on the pay of men and women and the fight against discrimination.
in writing. i believe, of course, that cuba should be a parliamentary democracy. i believe, however, that foreign policy is a national concern and that multilateral channels, such as the un, are the only acceptable option for influencing countries outside the eu's immediate vicinity. i have voted against the resolution for the reasons stated above.
in writing. i have voted in favour of the proposed resolution on cuba, even though it is missing several important facts in the political context. the main problem in the cuban situation is caused by the us-lead blockade and the aggressive us threats against cuba. putting an end to the blockade and stopping the aggressive threats by the us would be the most important step in creating an atmosphere in which there would be better possibilities to create a true democracy in cuba. but the aggressive us policy is not the only reason for the severe restrictions on freedom of expression and democracy in cuba. the cuban government also has its own individual responsibility to bear. one example, among others, is the decision not to let the sakharov prize-winners, the damas de blanco, leave the country to receive the prize in strasbourg. i am voting in favour of the resolution, despite its imperfections, since i want to protest against the cuban government’s travel ban on the damas de blanco.
the committee on the environment, public health and food safety has tabled an opinion. president. - the debate is closed. the committee on the environment welcomes the commission's proposal for common rules for chickens for fattening, in particular because it brings clear improvements in animal welfare, but also because it promotes the functioning of the internal market. the major problem with the commission's proposal is that it is too low in terms of animal welfare. for example, according to the conclusions of the scientific committee on animal health and animal welfare, which we have used as a basis for our decision, the level of occupancy must be less than 25 kg in order to avoid major deficiencies and unnecessary suffering. in line with this, many of us have tabled amendments on the degree of occupancy and on the points system for damage to the tram ducts. these changes are necessary to ensure the protection of animals. we in the european parliament must take the expertise seriously and show the council that we want to tighten up the commission's proposals on animal welfare. that is the most important thing at the moment, and perhaps not the details of our amendments. i therefore hope that a majority of this parliament will vote in favour of these amendments tomorrow, which have therefore received a large amount of support in the committee on the environment. a large majority voted for them, both from the right and from the left.
madam president, i would like to thank you. no other animal is suffering as much as broiler chickens. it's the animals that are in the worst shape today. they are bred to extreme growth that causes one-fifth of all these animals to have joint damage. packed tight, stressed, and born without the closeness of their mother, they suffer their short lives. berman has tried to find a good balance of measures, but unfortunately the committee on agriculture has a short-term interest page. this must be rectified by the proposals of the committee on the environment. both animal health and our consumers demand better conditions. i thank jørgensen and others for their cooperation. according to eurobarometer, the single animal welfare issue most europeans want to tackle is precisely improving the conditions of broilers. today, parliament must listen to their voices. therefore, vote in favour of the proposals of the verts/ale group and those put forward by mr jørgensen and the rest of us in the committee on the environment. without these improvements, the industry loses all credibility and risks being knocked out by low consumer confidence rather than by competing imports.
member of the commission. mr president, i would like to thank members for a very interesting debate. perhaps i should have quoted mahatma ghandi earlier: ‘the greatness of a nation and its moral progress can be judged by the way its animals are treated’. in the european union, thank god, we have a very high level of civilisation. the largest number of letters and complaints i have received from european citizens concerns animal welfare, in particular broilers. so this proposal reflects the views of european citizens. it is based on science, but also on consultations with all the stakeholders over a period of three years to take account of all their views and concerns. it is a compromise. some would like us to go further and have said it is a low-standard proposal. others say we have gone too far. it is a step towards protecting the welfare of animals, but it also takes into account the interests of the producers. competitiveness is certainly important, but we know that the cost imposed on production by animal welfare standards is very low, something between 2.5 and 8 cents per bird. the other costs affect competitiveness. but i do not believe that european producers can compete on the basis of cost. they should compete on the basis of quality. i have some photographs here of the dermatitis caused by this intensive farming and i do not think that any european consumer would be willing to eat these birds; it is just that they do not know about it. so awareness and education are the best policy, together with labelling, which is very important. we have letters; we have eurobarometer. european citizens are ready to pay more, provided they know, and we are working on that. on the issue of avian flu, best practices in animal welfare help us implement preventive measures more effectively, to protect the industry and the birds from avian flu. if it is easier to detect sick animals, it is easier to control the situation, and therefore these proposals on animal welfare and protection against avian flu are compatible. regarding the amendments, because of the lack of time we will circulate a full list of those that have been accepted and those that have not. i would ask that this list be included in the verbatim report of proceedings of this sittingcommission’s position on amendments by parliament: see ‘position of the commission’ annex. many of the amendments were accepted because they are compatible with the philosophy of the proposal. others cannot be accepted, mostly for technical reasons or because the provisions are already included in existing legislation. proposals such as those that take account of climatic conditions, types of production, training of staff and the way the checks should be performed can all be accepted. one of the most sensitive issues was density. we feel that the proposal is a reasonable compromise. it meets the standards, it improves the situation and it establishes conditions and controls. we would therefore prefer that parliament accept the commission’s proposal on that point and we cannot accept that amendment. concerning mandatory labelling, we feel – and i agree with mr parish that it is very important – that six months is a rather short period. it would be very difficult to produce a substantial report with specific proposals, so we need more time for that. finally, on the issue of cost, not everything can be measured in monetary terms, but i know it is a very important aspect. animal welfare is a very small part of the production cost. i mentioned earlier that in one of the cases the world bank studied, with the adoption of a very small number of improvements in the treatment of birds, a farm managed to save usd 320 000 per year. i think that proves that adopting animal welfare policies is only really a cost in the short term; eventually it makes a profit and there is a financial benefit for the producers. i would like to thank members again for their support for this proposal, which is a big step towards improving animal welfare in the european union.
rapporteur. – mr president, the roadmap for state aid reform from the commission is a good and an important document. i hope that this report will contribute to the improvement of the state aid policy. therefore, i should like to thank colleagues and shadow rapporteurs who have contributed to this improvement by the amendments and discussions we have had in the committee on economic and monetary affairs. i hope there will be, as there was in the committee, broad support for this new policy, which i hope will make an impact on the future guidelines on state aid policy that the commission will be responsible for. herr talman, europa behöver ett konkurrenskraftigt näringsliv. vi behöver nya företag, nya jobb, nya produkter, nya marknader, vilket vi säger väldigt ofta. det är därför också viktigt att följa upp detta genom att se till att nya och små företag får lika bra förutsättningar som gamla och stora företag. vi vet mycket väl genom olika erfarenheter att statsstöd alltid tenderar att slå vakt om de gamla existerande företagen på de nya och växande företagens bekostnad. om vi ser oss omkring i europeisk ekonomi liksom i internationell ekonomi, ser vi mycket tydligt att de nya företagen och de nya produkterna inte har kommit fram genom statsstöd utan genom goda förutsättningar för företagande och enskilda entreprenörer. varje typ av statsstöd som snedvrider konkurrensen tenderar alltid att snedvrida den på bekostnad av det nya och växande samt till fördel för det gamla. detta är därför en fråga som i högsta grad handlar om hur vi skall få nya jobb och nya företag. en statsstödspolitik som innebär att vi konserverar företag med liten konkurrenskraft, gamla produkter och låga löner kommer att bidra till att europa släpar efter. en politik som däremot riktar in sig på mindre statsstöd, på att ta bort sådana statsstöd som snedvrider konkurrens och som istället satsar resurser på innovation och forskning kommer att kunna ge ett annat resultat. vi har i europeisk historia mycket att lära från en lång rad olika erfarenheter när det gäller statsstöd. jag skriver i mitt betänkande att kommissionen noggrant bör följa och analysera dessa erfarenheter, liksom de goda erfarenheter som finns, eftersom målet för statsstöd ofta är populärt och allmänt gillat. problemet är bara att resultatet sällan blir det som man har tänkt sig. resultatet blir att man hamnar i bidragsberoende och i gamla strukturer samt att vi inte får fram de nya arbetstillfällena. vi har sett det inom varvs- och stålindustri och inom en lång rad andra sektorer. där statsstöd går in, går förnyelsen och förändringen ut. vi måste se till att situationen blir den omvända. ett övergripande mål i betänkandet är att följa upp det generella målet att minska statsstöd men också, vilket skrivs tydligt i betänkandet och ännu tydligare i kommissionens dokument, att ta bort sådant statsstöd som snedvrider konkurrens. ytterligare en målsättning är att se till att de regler som finns skall bli tydligare. målet måste nämligen vara att följa upp kravet på mindre statsstöd. i fråga om de krav som ställs på statsstöd gäller att det skall vara förutsägbart, det skall inte kunna användas till omlokaliseringar, reglerna för statsstöd skall vara generella, stödet skall ha en generell inriktning, det skall vara horisontellt inriktat och inte gynna det ena företaget på det andras bekostnad. detta är väldigt viktigt när vi diskuterar innovationer. innovationspolitiken får nämligen inte bli en ursäkt för ökat statsstöd, utan den skall syfta till att skapa bättre förutsättningar för innovation. samma sak gäller företag i allmänhetens intresse. vi får inte ha ett statsstöd som överfinansierar den typen av verksamheter, utan vi måste ge möjligheter åt nya företag att växa fram. detsamma kan sägas om det regionala stödet eller miljöstödet. det måste ha en horisontell riktning, vilket gör det öppet och tillgängligt för alla. om vi ser till att så sker och följer upp kraven på ökad insyn och öppenhet, bättre redovisning och starkare kontroll, då kommer vi att lägga grunden för en statsstödspolitik som innebär mindre statsstöd, inget statsstöd som snedvrider konkurrens, däremot ökade resurser för innovationer, bättre miljö och levande regioner.
for the alde group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. i would like to begin by urging the commission, as in this own-initiative report, to submit a legislative proposal on the share of renewable energy in the heating and cooling sector as soon as possible. there is broad support here in parliament for what such a directive should contain. we are happy to complement the two directives on the promotion of renewable energy sources in electricity and transport with the third sector, heating, as the commission's ambition in the white paper 'energy for the future: renewable energy sources'. a community strategy and action plan' from 1997. almost half of europe's total energy consumption is used for heating. with today's warning signs of climate change and given our dependence on imports of traditional energy sources with high carbon emissions as a result, it is of the utmost importance that we exploit the enormous potential and existing resources that we have in renewable energy sources in europe. by increasing the shares of these in the heating and cooling sector, we have great gains to make. let me mention a few: we're reducing the carbon footprint. we are reducing our dependence on imports and increasing self-sufficiency in europe. we are not as easily affected by energy crises elsewhere in the world. by promoting renewable sources, we are providing incentives for innovation and technological development in this area, which in turn leads to further more efficient and cleaner energy consumption. by choosing renewable sources in accordance with the conditions of each member state, we contribute to regional development. we are creating more jobs; it is one of the few areas in europe where we can actually see that we are creating new jobs and moving in the right direction towards increased growth. these are the needs and the benefits, but how do we get there? i believe that national binding targets for the share of renewable energy are a first step, but it is not enough. i would also like to ask the commission to create the conditions necessary for companies to have the courage to make long-term investments in technologies that allow greater use of renewable energy sources, and to give the commission the resources necessary for the development and discovery of new technologies for the crucial research. finally, i would like to draw attention to the technological solutions that already exist and that enable us to make more efficient use of the energy we use to heat homes, namely district heating. technical solutions and the choice of energy source go hand in hand. modern and technologically advanced district heating is part of the solution and can also be combined with electricity generation in the so-called trigeneration. this own-initiative report clearly and unambiguously sets out what parliament wants to see in a future legislative proposal. i hope that the commission will follow up this initiative quickly, so that we can quickly have the incentives needed to switch to an increasingly renewable energy source to heat or cool europe. it takes a new way of thinking to solve the problems that we created with the old way of thinking, as albert einstein once said. parliament has done a great deal of work in this area. we now hope that the commission will continue this work.

rapporteur. – mr president, i tend to agree with you that parts two and three do not make sense following the rejection of part one. it is not one of my amendments, but i would say that the vote we already had is enough. (laughter)
rapporteur. – i would like to move the following oral amendment, which has been agreed with the shadow rapporteur of the pse group: ‘whereas state aid which does not distort competition is a permissible means of promoting economic development and can thus be one means of promoting the lisbon agenda for economic growth and jobs, alongside other tools, including community funding, such as the structural funds’.
in writing. the report raises the question of the value of the internal market in relation to national self-determination. a case is currently pending before the court of justice (case c-156/04). we do not think that a statement here and now by the european parliament will in any way advance the case. we have therefore voted against this report, but we would like to emphasize that we are not taking a position on the substance of the current debate. the court of justice may first take a position and give a ruling. the greek authorities and the national parliament will then discuss how to deal with the court's decisions.
in writing. i voted against this proposal at first reading, but now the governments of the member states have improved it. this directive does not imply any new legislation but merely the application of existing legislation in areas where the eu has already assumed competence. the bizarre proposal to include solar radiation has been eliminated. in addition, the rules are based on international conventions and, where these are lacking, national legislation is accepted. health checks and follow-up shall be carried out in accordance with national practice. this is therefore a reasonably balanced proposal which can be accepted. the benefits of occupational safety and health outweigh the problem of further legislation, as this directive does not in practice lead to any new eu competence.
in writing. we have voted to the best of our ability for better animal husbandry. however, some amendments put to the vote have been too detailed for political decision-makers to take a position on. the details of the nighttime, foot injuries and first week mortality of a broiler are difficult for a layman to determine. we would have welcomed it if we could have decided on general principles and then had experts draw up the more precise regulatory framework. this is a consultation procedure for the european parliament and not a co-decision procedure, which could have allowed for a more principled statement on the matter. we also want to stress the importance of moving forward in negotiations within the world trade organisation to reach a global consensus on stricter production requirements in terms of food safety and animal welfare requirements.
in writing. i voted for this very weak report because it does, after all, bring about improvements in most countries and is about minimum legislation, which means that we are allowed to have stricter rules. our amendments on reducing congestion were very reasonable compromises which received almost unanimous support in the committee on the environment; it is tragic that they did not receive support. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of animal by-products. this is shooting yourself in the foot for the industry. i voted for the agriculture committee's proposal on 34 kg in 2013 because it is better than the original proposal for that reason alone. this can be used against the council where several countries want to go over 100 pounds. the european parliament is at any rate taking a different approach. chickens will continue to suffer more than any other animal. the way we treat our animals says a lot about how advanced our civilization is. this case says we're barbarians.
in writing. an analysis of state aid must be based on a clear conception of the differences between private and public revenues and costs, i.e. on a conception of the existence of externalities. this report goes beyond the analysis of principle and thus comes to general conclusions which cannot be substantiated. in addition, external effects will be used in the debate without any serious attempt at evaluation. we have nevertheless voted in favour of the report because it generally discourages state aid. it is common experience that state aid is used to an excessive extent for protectionist reasons.
in writing. the report on the effects of globalisation on the single market was voted for by the moderates today. we support the report's view that globalisation will require economic reforms to maintain europe's competitiveness. however, we are critical of the establishment of a globalisation fund, as the consequences of globalisation are best managed by a dynamic market economy.
in writing. the report deals with the human rights clause in agreements concluded by the european union. an example of such an agreement is the cotonou agreement concluded by the eu with the acp countries. the clause has been in place for ten years, and the rapporteur now believes it appropriate to evaluate it. at present, the european parliament cannot enter into negotiations if it considers that a country has breached the clause. among the proposals made by agnoletto is that parliament should be able to initiate this type of dialogue. the rapporteur also wants the european parliament to be involved in the whole procedure concerning the clause. the above shows that parliament is being given greater powers, which we are opposed to in principle. that is why we voted against the agnoletto report in today's vote.
in writing. the junilists are opposed to the eu's common fisheries policy and want to end this policy in the long term. we therefore believe that the eu should not decide whether to keep the shetland box and the red-spotted box. this question can be decided with advantage by existing international bodies.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the proposal for a services directive is reactionary. it threatens the rights of workers and risks leading to social dumping. we therefore call for the motion to be rejected in its entirety. if that does not happen, we will vote for any proposal that limits the harmful effects of the directive, such as proposals to remove the country of origin principle and proposals to exclude sectors from the directive. the compromise proposal between the pse and ppe groups removes important shortcomings from the proposal, but there are still major ambiguities which will be settled in the ec court of justice. we do not want the rights of the workers to be decided there. we cannot accept that the rights of workers and the legislation which is supposed to protect our citizens are sacrificed on the altar of the free market.
mr president, i would like to thank you. for almost two years we have been discussing this services directive throughout europe. it's great that there's finally something that people are engaging in amongst the things that we do in this congregation. sometimes there are many misunderstandings, and sometimes these misunderstandings are exploited in a rather distasteful way. the services directive aims to remove all the thousands of bureaucratic obstacles that hinder europe's entrepreneurs, not least small businesses. we must not forget that it is the companies that create jobs. without companies, there are no employees at all. the service sector is a growing part of our economies. there are great opportunities for jobs and growth. we need to open up and reform the european economies, which is quite urgent. the country of origin principle is a clever one in that it creates a genuine internal market without discrimination. this is a major step forward for citizens and businesses. the principle was clarified and specified in the imco compromise, which states that the host country's rules on labour law, public health and safety apply. the compromise now circulating among the major groups has introduced a very clumsy concept, namely social policy. this is deeply unfortunate, because it opens the door to protectionism and a great many different legal interpretations. we are already hearing how representatives of the various groups interpret this. but now it seems that that compromise is dying, which we should welcome. the services directive is about how europe will cope in a globalised world. it is about growth, the economy, employment and freedom of choice. since the treaty of rome, we have had the aim of establishing free movement also for services and now is the time.
mr president, i would like to thank you. parliament is capitulating today, renouncing its political role. the compromise is to remove the concept of the country of origin principle and replace it with a political vacuum. this vacuum will then be filled by the court of justice, which will in turn reinstate the principle of the country of origin, since it consistently puts the internal market before everything else. the court of justice may not be removed from office or held liable. is that democracy? only large companies with armies of lawyers will be able to assert their interests under the services directive. the only unemployment that the directive will remedy is that of the lawyers. the losers will be the municipalities, the public servants, the consumers and the small businesses. let us vote against this directive! instead of forcing the liberalization of everything, we should respect democratically made decisions to preserve sectors from short-term market thinking. the eu cannot live by economic efficiency alone. we also need democratic efficiency, where citizens are not constantly overwhelmed by bad directives.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the swedish junilista is committed to the proper functioning of the internal market and therefore has a positive attitude towards the services directive. at the same time, we value national self-determination. there must be good reasons for member states to delegate power and competence to the eu. if we accept the principle of the country of origin, we are giving up national sovereignty. we believe that the benefits of this principle are too limited for us to be prepared to do so. this principle mainly concerns services such as construction, cleaning and consultancy. these are important sectors, but they do not have a decisive influence on sweden's prosperity and gdp. it is also positive that national service monopolies will not be included in the directive. if these are to be reorganised, it must be done in a democratic spirit, that is, through a broad debate in those countries which consider such a change desirable. we will support the compromise.
mr president, i would like to thank you. at the moment, everyone's attention is on the european parliament. we all have the opportunity to set the political agenda. we shall take advantage of this opportunity to put our foot down on this extremely important directive. this is not the bolkestein directive but a compromise which is emerging, which is something else entirely. i will give you some examples from the employment committee. employment law, collective agreements and the right to take action are not affected by the directive. the member states will now be able to require service companies to have a representative who can conclude collective agreements, be responsible for health and safety inspections, etc. public services such as health care, social services and education services are excluded from the directive. the staffing companies are not affected either, pending a specific directive on the staffing companies. in the case of services of general economic interest, it is up to the member states to open them up to competition, but if they do so, this should also apply to the whole of the internal market. this is a good compromise, combining the benefits of the single market with labour market security and protecting the public services that citizens in our member states and regions have access to today. some consider this to be an unclear compromise. but what about today? what happens if we reject the directive? how many cases are waiting for a decision in the courts instead of being decided by a directive where we lay down the rules of the game? i say that this directive is moving forward! it is clearer than the regulations we have today, and that is why we should vote in favour.
the commission is not prepared to accept this. this is a giant leap for the eu and a small step for free trade. whether there is a step forward depends on the vote on thursday. the exceptions are too many, and the principle must not be too weak. it is important for small businesses, and it is especially important for small countries. without added value, the compromise is worthless. the commission therefore has a special responsibility in the further work. protectionism within the eu, which is disguised as social policy or consumer protection, is unreasonable. let me give you two examples. if you are to build 25 identical villas in germany, you must submit 25 plans for approval to the authorities and pay 25 times, even though it is the exact same villa that is to be built. does that make sense? no, it's expensive for the consumer and unreasonable! if a swedish tourist group goes on a holiday to greece and has a diving instructor with them, they must speak greek. otherwise he is not allowed to work with and speak to a swedish group in greece, even though this swedish group does not know any greek at all. this is ridiculous! we therefore need better rules for crossborder trade in services. we can improve this compromise on thursday by reducing exemptions and also including private health care. so push through what the austrian presidency says it is seeking, namely an ambitious services directive that will contribute to increased prosperity for all of us.
mr president, i would like to thank you. after listening to the debate here in the plenary, it is worth recalling why we should have a services directive and why we are working towards a more open trade in services. this is because we all agree on the basic principle that increased trade in services leads to more jobs, more new businesses, more growth and better competitiveness. however, when we hear the debate here, it seems that many have forgotten this. free trade in services is not a negative thing, it is a positive thing. unfortunately, the opponents have succeeded in cutting back on the directive. this means that we will be less competitive than we could have been otherwise. that means fewer new jobs and fewer new businesses. that is not what the citizens of europe expect. i will support the compromise, along with other moderates, not because that is what we want but because the compromise is a step along the way. i would like to stress, however, that i do not accept the picture that is being presented, namely that there is a conflict between east and west, between the new and the old member states. instead, it is a dividing line between those who want more european integration and cooperation, more open borders and more and new jobs through trade in services and those who disagree. we will work to bring about private health, that the posting directive is clearly open to all, and that we do not discriminate against citizens of any country. we will also work to ensure that staffing companies are included in this activity. i would like to say the following to the commission: this is a first step, you have a responsibility to ensure that we take further steps in the direction that the treaty provides.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the situation in iran is indeed a cause for concern. not only is the human rights situation deplorable, but the regime is also deceiving the international community by violating international principles and ignoring the demands of the iaea. the idea that iran could acquire nuclear weapons is, of course, extremely frightening, not least in light of the statements and threats against israel and what that would mean for the rest of the region. moreover, the regime in tehran seems to be perfectly suited to the unrest surrounding the drawings of muhammad, and there are many indications that the unrest is being fomented by tehran itself. it is time for the eu to formulate a very clear strategy towards iran. it should include several different points: clear support for dissidents, democratic forces inside and outside iran, such as the referendum movement, strong condemnation of the gross violations of human rights, pressure to comply with international conventions and international cooperation, and of course dialogue and diplomacy. however, this strategy should also include preparations for possible sanctions decided by the un security council.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the debate we are having is very important. the debate in this parliament and in the media on the middle east has mostly been concentrated on the palestinian elections and on iraq. now, with regard to iran, the nuclear issue is being discussed. at the same time, human rights are being violated, as many of the previous speakers have pointed out. as a member of the committee on the interior and the committee on foreign affairs and as vice-president of the socialist women's party in europe, i receive very disturbing reports and stories of violations of the rights of both women and children. human rights issues must be given greater prominence. we should be acting much more forcefully than i think we are today. big changes in iranian society are happening right now. we are too few to talk about dialogue and too many to be stuck in the big conflict issue. the dialogue between the eu and iran must be resumed. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament. diplomatic solutions must be put before an escalation of violence and violations. in modern conflicts, it is always civilians, women and children, who are the first to be harmed and violated. it must be stopped now while there is still time to do it.
in writing. the junilist fully supports the iaea's efforts to reach a peaceful solution to the confrontation between iran and the international community and considers that the un security council has the ultimate responsibility to decide what action should be taken. as iran is not part of the eu's immediate neighbourhood, this is not an eu issue unless the member states can reach unanimity in the council on a common position. sweden should work there to prevent iran from developing a nuclear weapon. however, this is not a matter for the european parliament to deal with. we have therefore decided not to attempt to amend the resolution and have voted against it in its entirety.
mr president, i would like to thank you. to understand why the situation in bosnia is so complicated today and why we need constitutional reform in bosnia, we need to understand the background. anyone who experienced the war in bosnia or who followed the events in the balkans in the 1990s knows that the daytona accords had a mission, namely to stop the war. it was imperative to cease fighting immediately and prevent the country from being forced to experience another winter of war. nor did anyone consider the solution offered by the daytona accords to be the perfect solution. on the contrary, there were many who felt that it was not good at all. the daytona accords should probably be seen more as the result of international efforts than as a desired compromise between the various political leaders of bosnia. since then, the peace agreement has preserved the territorial integrity of bosnia, but at the same time, annex 4 to the agreement stipulates that the country will be divided into two parts, a federation of bosniaks and croats and a serb part called republika srpska. ten years later, we find that the consequences of the daytona accords make it difficult for bosnia to function as a normal european country. then let us not even speak of hundreds of ministers or the unruly state apparatus. the main problem, in my view, is that the daytona agreement has led to a situation in which the interests of ethnic groups take precedence over those of individual citizens. the daytona accords thus completed the work begun by the nationalist parties in the first post-communist elections in 1990. these parties still have more or less absolute political power, and they share a more or less pronounced desire to dominate a certain territory inhabited by their respective ethnic groups. this is where the difficulty lies in agreeing on an economic or political future which will apply to all citizens. the difficulty of agreeing on a president, a customs system or an education system is also there. the ethnic has become, and still is, more important than the civil. i was born and raised in bosnia and have experienced the war there. i am absolutely convinced that, as was the case when the daytona accords were signed, the country must receive the necessary active help from the international community. this excellent resolution can be the first step in the eu's strong commitment to this work.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i am one of those in my group who believe that the group should have signed the joint motion for a resolution. i'm sorry that hasn't happened. the situation in belarus is getting worse. the conditions for a democratic election are unfortunately very poor. the opposition is being systematically persecuted. this applies to both the political opposition and the free trade union movement. critical, independent media have very little scope to operate. in this situation, we must do everything we can to support the democratic forces and to work for a fair election, including working for massive election monitoring. there is only one way to show solidarity with belarus, and that is to demand that the belarusians themselves decide their future. because it requires democracy.
member of the commission. ‘yes, we do’ – that would be the shortest answer. under the competitiveness and innovation framework programme, one phase addresses the issue at hand, and the other phases are addressed through the framework programme. from the very beginning, we have coordinated our activities, which are having a similar effect on innovation as the structural funds have on the regions. furthermore, when we talk about involving business in the work we are doing, it is crucial to underline that we have a new tool that did not exist in the past: i refer to the technology platforms, which were established at the beginning of this century and which are developing very fast. the whole idea of technology platforms – i believe we currently have 28 of them – is that they are bottom-up initiatives, starting with the business’s point of view. in these platforms, the points of view of practically all stakeholders are taken on board. so, while these platforms stem from a business initiative, they also include researchers, politicians, ngos, financial institutions, regulatory authorities and the others on board. they create what are known as ‘strategic research agendas’ for the next 20 years – or more, depending on various factors. this is the first time that this kind of tool and this kind of logic have been developed at european union level. i meet daily with industrialists and with others, who are clearly very enthusiastic about what is going on. even if we were now to remove the incentives, which were, at the outset – if we are frank – financed through the framework programme, this is now a process with its own story. it will definitely make a change at european union level. i believe that we are part of a process that will produce hugely important benefits. on the basis of their views for the long-term future, where you have an idea of what research should be undertaken, to the final product and the business sector’s ideas on how to evaluate it in their work, we take this input on board when we create our cooperation activities. in practice, that is in the major part of our programmes. moreover, in some areas, where we believe that this long-term, strong partnership exists, and where there is strong commitment from the companies and other actors, such as member states, for instance, we are ready to develop what are known as ‘joint technological initiatives’, which are a new long-term tool with clear cofinancing, as a new tool, from the european commission. we have never had such a clear overview of the intentions, strategies and points of view of the business sector as we have today when we create the framework programme, and, of course, in the final phase, the specific work programmes.
member of the commission. mrs wallström is in bed with the flu. that is the reason. as a complement to the commission’s reply to written question e-4200/05, asked previously by the honourable member, on the issue of the goodwill ambassadors, the commission can reassure the honourable member that its intention during the period of reflection is to listen to citizens’ views on a range of eu issues and to draw conclusions from the debates held at all levels. the issues discussed will be very varied and they may or may not include recommendations on the desirable level of integration at eu level, but the role of the union will certainly be tackled and in any case the content of the database of debates will be reflected in the feedback process. this process will start with an initial synthesis report that will be presented by the commission to the european council under the austrian presidency in order to prepare the stocktaking exercise at the june 2006 european council. the commission would like to take this opportunity to remind the honourable member that the declaration calling for a period of reflection was adopted by the heads of state and government themselves, therefore it is for the council to take stock of the exercise as well. moreover, in the organisation of national debates the european institutions have an important role to play, but the primary responsibility rests with the member states. what the commission has recommended in plan d is that the national debates need to be structured to ensure that the feedback can have a direct impact on the policy agenda of the european union. concerning the second part of the honourable member’s question, the commission would like to stress that the different initiatives suggested by plan d at european level are part of a long-term plan and are being implemented at a different pace in the 25 member states. the european goodwill ambassador plan is one of the measures that need to be further developed in most of the countries of the eu and in cooperation with the member states, depending on the creativity and the proposals identified by those involved in the process. there is no single model for every country, just as there is no standard model for the organisation of the debates in the member states. in any case, the commission thinks that it is important to reiterate that the ambassadors would always act on a volunteer basis and the views that they would convey would be their own views. the commission has no intention of imposing a particular line on them.

member of the commission. mr president, i wish to begin by thanking all those who have contributed to this debate. many of the points mentioned in the report are covered by the ongoing studies that the commission has initiated, for example, proposals 2, 5, 7, 19 and 23. regarding the safety-net provision – proposal 8 – the discussion in the council has not produced a clear mandate for introducing a general safety clause in all coms or for trying to finance this insurance out of the modulated money. however, i am ready to examine the introduction of targeted risk and crisis management provisions in other coms and, as mentioned by mr gklavakis, in the fruit and vegetable sector, which we will also discuss here in parliament at the end of this year. i completely agree with your rapporteur, mr graefe zu baringdorf, that basic coverage against income reduction is not a solution. it is quite clear that, if or when we face structural problems, they must be solved through policy changes. that is obvious and i am very happy about mr graefe zu baringdorf’s clear support for it. as a safety-net for income, i consider the cap reform’s decoupled direct payment to the farmer to be essential. i am positive about the content of proposals 18, 24 and 26. i also take this opportunity to draw your attention to the review of the state aid guidelines adopted by the commission on 8 february. the commission proposes to include compensation for bad weather and animal and plant diseases in the present exemption regulation for state aid within the agricultural sector. this is hopefully a simplification. it would also speed up the implementation of state aid in such situations, so that we can react extremely quickly. the commission has looked into a number of options for encouraging the development of risk crisis management tools and providing an improved response in the event of a crisis. my intention for the communication was, firstly, to have a comprehensive orientation debate in the council and here in parliament before tabling any legislative proposal. the discussions we have had so far in the council have not, as i said, produced a clear mandate to introduce a general safety clause. however, the commission is ready to proceed further if there should at any stage be a clear mandate from the council. since i consider crisis and risk management an important issue for the future as well, even in the situation of an absence of a clear mandate from the council i am prepared to examine the introduction of a crisis management provision, as i said previously, on a case-by-case basis. there was a question about the position of the new member states. it is quite clear that the new member states will not be excluded from risk management measures. as we have pointed out in the communication, we could envisage identifying a comparable envelope in rural development, just as we do for the old member states – a clear signal to the ten new member states. with the political agreement on the cap reform and the implementation that has now taken place in all the member states, we want european farmers to try and seize the market opportunities. there is a strong responsibility for the farmer, but there is also a need for solidarity when crises arise. listening to the discussions in the european parliament as well as in the council, it seems to me that the approaches are fairly different. my conclusion is, therefore, that we need to study the options carefully and discuss them before taking a final decision on the instruments to be used. i agree completely with mr graefe zu baringdorf that prevention is usually better than cure and my personal view is that the conference on coexistence to be held in april is necessary in order to avoid gmos being mixed with organic production. i am therefore particularly looking forward to that discussion. as for your views on fossil fuels, i do not think that we should wait until we have used up those fuels; we should start now to look at what can be done on renewable energy. i am thinking specifically about our recent discussions on bioethanol and the possibility of taking full advantage of agriculture’s contribution to this future way of reducing our carbon dioxide emissions. this has been an interesting discussion and i thank the rapporteur for what i consider to be a balanced approach to this very important issue.
member of the commission. mr president, first of all i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, mr kindermann, on a very well-balanced report. the commission believes that the main issues and challenges in the european forestry sector are well reflected in the draft opinion. in particular, we appreciate the overall support given to the commission’s proposal concerning the preparation of a european action plan for forestry. in our view, the action plan should provide a coherent, verifiable and practical framework for the implementation of this strategy. it could become a major instrument of coordination between community actions and the forestry policies in the different member states. for that reason, we consider the development and implementation of the action plan a dynamic process, which should involve the member states and the stakeholders and should be accompanied by regular monitoring and reporting. the ideas are very well expressed in the draft opinion and we fully share the rapporteur’s view on the need to develop the action plan in close cooperation with the member states and the stakeholders. i think we have already made progress in that respect. the action plan will be based on the overall principles of the european forestry strategy, which are sustainability and subsidiarity. there are three specific issues that i would like to highlight. first, the need to enhance the competitiveness of the forestry sector, in line with the lisbon objectives. there is growing concern about the economic viability of sustainable forestry in the european union in the context of globalisation. competitiveness is a key pillar of sustainable forest management and it is of crucial importance in maintaining the multiple benefits that forestry provides to society, including employment opportunities in rural areas where there is nothing else. forestry can deliver a wide range of products and services to society and we should try to develop this potential in the future. the second issue is that the forestry sector has to respond to social demands concerning the protection of the environment and the natural heritage, in accordance with the gothenburg objectives of sustainable development. the forestry sector as a whole will prosper best if it fits snugly into wider society. actively contributing to major european objectives, such as biodiversity, conservation or climate change mitigation, will offer new opportunities for forestry. in relation to a previous discussion, the possibility of biomass for energy is a clear example of how forestry can contribute. the last issue is the global importance of forestry for sustainable development and the need firmly to continue our support for international commitments for the protection and sustainable management of forestry worldwide. that also involves measures to combat illegal logging and related trade in forest products. those issues are well addressed in the report and i can assure you that they will be reflected in our action plans.
the committee on the environment, public health and food safety has tabled an opinion. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. as rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on the environment, i would like to stress the incredible importance of forests for our continent and the fact that they affect a great many people. in sweden, forests and forestry account for nearly 30 percent of the swedish economy, and in europe there are over 15 million forest owners. what we decide here in the house therefore has a great deal of significance for many people, both in sweden and throughout europe. i know that there is a great deal of concern that the eu will intervene and start deciding, and that it will start to govern and decide on forestry issues from brussels. i can understand your concern. because that wouldn't be good. there are huge differences between an olive grove in southern italy and an industrial forestry in northern sweden. decisions about forests should be made locally, close to the people who know the forests, who use and own them. i would like to reassure those who fear such a development. i think the commission is very clear in its communication: forestry policy should be based on subsidiarity. we have made that clear in the environment committee. however, this does not mean that the eu has no responsibility. on the contrary, the eu has a great responsibility to take account of forests in all its decision-making. the eu already makes a large number of policy decisions that directly and indirectly affect european forests. however, we in the committee on the environment feel that there is almost no coordination between the various proposals concerning forests. it is damaging both to the compatibility of environmental objectives and to the conditions for forestry. i would therefore like to emphasize that i believe the commission's main task in the future is to coordinate existing policies and see how they affect the forest. in addition, ex ante evaluations must be carried out in good time, which can take into account the impact of different policy decisions on european forests and the forestry sector. in conclusion, i would like to stress the importance of forestry. it is perhaps the food that most clearly creates both economic and environmental value. however, as the commission points out, there are problems with the competitiveness of eu forestry in the global open field, which can cost environmental values. the message that i and the committee on the environment are sending you today is that by taking account of the forests, the commission should be able to create good, stable conditions for the forestry industry and thus give europe both a better economy and a better environment.
in writing. we moderates believe that the charter of fundamental rights must always be respected, whatever the issue or the legislation. we are in favour of the constitutional treaty, which would have made this statute legally binding on all eu citizens. we do not believe that only the labour law aspects of this statute should be mentioned in the services directive and have therefore abstained on the questions which mention the statute.
in writing. today the european parliament is giving its opinion on how we can create the free movement of services in the eu - one of the four freedoms on which our common european cooperation is based. in an increasingly globalised world economy with ever-increasing competition, the european economy needs to take advantage of the areas where we are competitive. the service sector is one such area. the compromise reached by the ppe-de and pse groups has unfortunately opened up new possibilities for the enactment of further protectionism which hinders trade in services between neighbouring countries and is a particularly hard blow to the newest member states. i have, however, voted in favour of the watered-down proposal today, as it may be a first step towards what i hope will be a free movement of services, already subject to competition, worthy of the name of our common european internal market.
in writing. junilista shares the view that the internal market in services is not complete. we welcome the services directive and believe that service companies, regardless of their country of origin, should not be discriminated against in any eu country. the central question in the debate on the services directive is whether the country of origin principle (article 16) should be the guiding principle of the directive. the junilista values both the single market and national self-determination. the basic question is therefore: does the principle of the country of origin imply such decisive advantages that we are prepared to renounce national sovereignty? our answer to that question is no. the country of origin principle concerns important but very limited areas such as construction, installation and consultancy services. we welcome competition in these areas, but believe that it should be on a level playing field for all parties. we believe that swedish rules should apply on swedish territory. we are therefore supporting the so-called host country principle. we also believe that national monopolies should be respected. the form these will take will be decided by a broad discussion in the member states. it follows from the above that we have chosen to support the compromise proposed by the ppe-de and pse.
in writing. the services sector is important and i would like to see a major step forward in this important market. unfortunately, today's vote is a modest step, which i regret. the unholy alliance between the conservatives and the social democrats is creating protectionist belts, legal disputes and restricting the labour market. this is something that europe's workers and consumers will lose out on. i regret that we are signalling to the new member states that there is still an 'us' and 'them'. i voted with great hesitation for the proposal, which is still a small step forward for the labour market.
in writing. today the european parliament has voted on one of the most important legislative issues we have ever dealt with - the services directive. the services directive aims to remove the obstacles to two of the freedoms that have existed in the ec treaty since 1958, the freedom of establishment for service providers and the freedom of movement for services. the potential for trade in services is enormous, and sweden is one of the countries that has the most to gain from a well-functioning market for services. the debate on the services directive has been extensive. the swedish and european trade unions argue that the directive contributes to 'social dumping'. this is not true, since the directive does not deal with labour law issues. the employment aspects have also been clarified by parliament's decisions. i want a good services directive which eliminates administrative rules and other barriers to trade. i also want the scope of the directive to be as broad as possible. that is why i voted for the inclusion of areas such as private health care, staffing companies and services of general economic interest. today's decision in parliament is based in part on a broad compromise. compromise is rarely perfect, but it is often necessary to achieve the goal. i hope that the eu member states will succeed in reaching a decision on this later this year, so that we can move forward on this very important issue.
in writing. the european parliament's committee on agriculture's examination of the strategic guidelines for rural development under the consultation procedure contains many heartwarming proposals. however, the european parliament is constantly trying to establish control over the agricultural and regional policies of the member states. that's what we're doing to the negatives. we believe that the member states cannot be bound by comprehensive rural development target documents. we have every confidence that the national or regional parliaments of the member states will deal with this matter well. we therefore voted against the amendments which the european parliament wishes to table to the council document on strategic guidelines for rural development.
in writing. the fight against poverty is one of the major challenges that all countries must address. the junilista group, however, argues that the work must be financed from the national budget of each country. international aid or other worthy projects may in no circumstances lead to the replacement of national taxation rights by eu taxation rights. we therefore vote against the resolution.
in writing. we are of the opinion that the eu should not develop a common forestry policy. this policy area is a matter of national concern. we would have liked the european parliament to have said that the aim should be to keep forestry policy a matter for national decision-making. we were given some hearing on this in the committee on the environment when we were discussing this report. but unfortunately, in accordance with its usual elephant disease, the majority in the european parliament has drawn up a report which, among other things, implies that the sectoral community policy and the lisbon strategy have consequences for forests and which advocates that an 'objective report should examine the possibilities of establishing a separate legal basis for forests' in the eu treaties. we are firmly opposed to such slippages in the political statements of the european parliament. once and for all it should be made clear that forestry policy is a national area of decision-making and nothing else. we cannot vote for a report which has a more or less hidden agenda of introducing a forestry policy at eu level to be pursued with funds from the eu budget.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i would first like to thank the rapporteur for a generally good report; i share most of the points. the report contains a generally low-key but nevertheless clear criticism of the imf's structural programmes and of the conditions imposed on the recipient countries. this is crucial, since these policies have actually worsened poverty and increased social problems in many countries. it is therefore crucial that the report's emphasis on poverty reduction and the achievement of the millennium development goals be included as the overarching policy objectives of the institution. in the longer term, this type of institution should probably be part of the un system and a coherent development policy. i also share the criticism of the lack of democracy in the imf. the crucial point here is that the power of the developing countries must increase. perhaps most importantly, they must have a more reasonable share of the votes when decisions are taken. however, democratization must also mean that a developing country can take over the presidency. it will not automatically go to one of the richest countries. the democratic control within the eu should also be increased, but it should be exercised by the national parliaments. i do not think it is reasonable to hand over the power of policy in the imf to the eu institutions and therefore oppose amendment no 5. i believe that the policy of the world trade organisation is a terrible example of the lack of democratic control over eu policy. for many years there has been a superstition about deregulation and free currency speculation. today, most currency movements are purely speculative. the protection against this, both nationally and internationally, is essential to achieving macroeconomic stability. we have tabled our own amendment to increase transparency and hope that it will be adopted. this is in line with the demand for greater democracy in the imf. we will vote against the ppe-de group's proposals but for most of the pse group's.
member of the commission. mr president, i would simply like to reiterate my thanks to the rapporteur and the members of the committees who have contributed to the preparation of this report, which, as i said in my opening remarks, i find extremely useful. i stress the importance of the discussion which is taking place and which, in principle, is intended to be concluded at the fund assembly in singapore in september on a new allocation of quotas which, in turn, is linked to a new allocation of voting rights and, therefore, to the governance of the fund, which can be improved. i think it is important that the european voice should work together to set a clear direction on what this improvement in the governance of the fund and the representation of the different states, according to fair criteria, in the governing bodies of the fund should mean. i would stress the importance - and i think the vast majority of members will agree with me - of greater coordination between the countries of the european union in the positions of the european member states on the fund. as the coordination of economic policies progresses, as the integration of the different member states into the internal market, into economic and monetary union, deepens, and as we want this projection out of europe to have an economic dimension as well, it is important that this external economic dimension of the european union should also be reflected in the deliberations and discussions of the monetary fund. i believe that the role of the european parliament in relation to the monetary fund will advance as the coordination of the european voice in the institutions of the fund progresses, until at some point - i am convinced that it will be achieved, although not in the short term - a single voice and a single representation of the countries of the single european currency in the monetary fund. it is not for today, nor for tomorrow, but it is a direction which seems to me both inevitable and desirable. i would add, as a final comment, that reference has been made - and i share that concern - to the need for the imf to act in coordination with the strategies defined also in other areas by multilateral organizations and, in particular, by the organizations of the united nations system. i think we should be pleased because the imf is involved and committed to the achievement of the millennium development goals, which is a major objective of the international community. i believe that what might have been unthinkable 15 years ago is now a reality.
the imf's board of governors is composed of 25 people representing a single country or a group of countries. the eu countries are represented individually or as members of 9 of these groups. the nordic group is made up of the nordic countries and the baltic states. this includes countries such as norway and iceland which are not members of the eu. the role of the imf has changed since the imf was created shortly after world war ii. the main task from the outset was to help countries with current account problems to temporarily finance their current account deficits and to monitor the management of their economies within a fixed exchange rate system. it was also important that countries did not competitively devaluate their currencies. today, the euro countries, with the cb as their central bank, have a role similar to that of the imf. the euro countries have fixed exchange rates. any support loans to euro area countries in financial crisis may be managed within the eurosystem. countries outside the euro area have floating exchange rates and therefore do not run into exchange-rate imbalance problems. they also have better control over their debt ratios. the natural thing is that the euro countries form a common group in the imf with one seat. eu countries outside the euro area should not be included. nor is there any reason for sweden to join a common eu group. we would also lose our ability to actively influence the imf's relationship with developing countries.
for the verts/ale group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. this resolution is short and can be summed up in a single paragraph: close down the guantanamo. every form of human rights shines with its absence, and the whole invention is from beginning to end a matter of human error, not rights. in a single presidential term, the united states has gone from being a country that most people want to visit, trade with, and associate with to being a country that many people avoid. guantanamo may be a military base in cuba, but it's a cowardly attempt to evade the laws of the united states. we ask nothing less than that the united states abide by its own constitution and that the conventions of the united nations be obeyed. in either case, guantanamo is an absurdity. the bush regime is acting like an old-fashioned colonial autocrat in terms of the kyoto protocol, the international criminal court, abu ghraib, the war on the people of iraq and a host of other conventions covering everything from tobacco control to the right to food. these departures from international cooperation and international law are hurting the american people and the world at large. guantanamo detainees are not alone in suffering the oppression of the bush regime, even if they suffer the most. we also sympathize with the majority of americans who never supported the bush regime and who are victims of its arbitrary procedures. these ordinary citizens are the victims of the world's hatred and threats. the first step toward making amends for the bush regime would be to close guantanamo.

questioned by the police. president. - the debate is closed. millions of people worldwide are looking forward to the forthcoming world cup, but how many thousands of women will be sacrificed so that men will not only watch football but also be able to buy readily available sexual services? in order to provide sufficient sexual services, tens of thousands of women must, often under duress, supplement the already large german group of prostituted women. this trafficking is unworthy and deeply inhuman, and it shows an oppressive view of women and a debasement of all the men who come to watch football. in order to stop violence against women and children, the responsibility for sex trafficking and prostitution must not be placed on the victims, but on those who take the right to buy and trade women's bodies. forced prostitution can be interpreted as the opposite, i.e. some form of so-called voluntary prostitution. but there is no voluntary prostitution; women do not choose prostitution they are forced into it for various reasons. it may be by becoming a victim of organized crime, but poverty and unemployment are other causes. but above all, there is a clear link between prostitution and the woman's past experience of physical, psychological and sexual abuse. the links between the legalization of prostitution and the growing sex-slave trade must be highlighted. what does the legalization of prostitution in germany mean for the increase in the number of victims of sex slavery in connection with the world cup, for example? legislation that allows prostitution must be evaluated and compared with, for example, the swedish legislation that criminalizes the client. swedish legislation has shown that it reduces demand and thus also the number of victims of trafficking and prostitution. the responsibility is put in the right place, it's put with the customer. they're the ones who have to take responsibility for their sexuality without buying women's bodies. if we accept that men take the right to buy women's bodies, then we also have to accept that some four million women and children are being transported across and between countries for sexual exploitation. condemning sexual exploitation and prostitution does not mean blaming women for prostitution. on the contrary! we want to give all women the right to their sexuality without oppression and on equal terms. women are not commodities. women are not for sale.
