in writing. we voted in favour of the report on the switchover from analogue to digital broadcasting. however, it is naive to think that the transition will not entail increased costs for consumers. the transition entails certain costs, such as new equipment. however, member states should ensure that this cost is kept at an acceptable level. it is important to combat piracy, but we are reluctant to continue to harmonise copyright.
in writing. we have voted against the report on the switchover from analogue to digital broadcasting because we do not believe that such decisions should be taken at eu level. we have also raised specific objections to the substance of the matter. cultural diversity cannot be guaranteed by governments or by protecting so-called public service enterprises from competition. we also oppose the idea of changing television technology by decree and political decision, rather than allowing the citizens to make their own choices.
in writing. this own-initiative report is about the future of eu-latin america cooperation. the european parliament is currently organising an interparliamentary conference, but now it wants to set up a parliamentary assembly with a permanent secretariat. the report contains a number of very bad proposals in areas where the european parliament should definitely not have a say. the eu will also support the development of the eu's external relations, including the establishment of a solidarity fund and a 'plan for latin america'. the junilist is opposed to this proposal. it is true that a very large proportion of the population of latin america lives in poverty, but the eu is not the solution to the problem. where appropriate, assistance should be provided by individual member states or through un agencies. it also proposes that the latin american regional parliaments should be elected as soon as possible, by direct universal suffrage, to create a structure similar to that of the european parliament. it is, of course, up to the states concerned to decide how their interparliamentary cooperation is to be organised. i therefore voted against the report in today's vote.
in writing. it is important to give priority to road safety issues and to work towards reducing the number of road deaths. there are therefore good reasons for member states to consider setting up the ecall in-vehicle emergency call service. however, i see no reason for the european parliament and the eu institutions to call on the member states to sign the ecall memorandum of understanding. the member states will have to take a position on this without political input from the eu institutions. the introduction of ecall is associated with costs and technical harmonisations. it must be up to each member state to assess which road safety measures are most urgent, realistic and prioritised. i have therefore voted against this report.
in writing. junilista has pointed out in this parliament before, and is doing so again, that the member states have exclusive responsibility for the organisation of education and the content of education systems. in the two resolutions under discussion, the european parliament is quietly trying to address language teaching and the adaptation of education systems to new technologies and digitalisation, among other things. i therefore vote 'no' to the two resolutions. however, i understand that member states may need to discuss among themselves some issues such as the consequences of the switchover from analogue to digital broadcasting. as usual, however, the european parliament goes too far in its proposals in its ambition to develop policies at eu level in all possible subject areas. i believe that the council of ministers is the best place to determine how far cooperation in the above-mentioned area needs to go.
i would like to thank the rapporteurs, mr scheele and mrs poli bortone, the shadow rapporteurs, the commission and the council for the work they have done to ensure that tomorrow we have an agreement to take a position on which will make it much easier for consumers to make healthier choices because they will have access to more fair and comprehensive information. many of us have probably bought breakfast cereal, bread, or yogurt that we have perceived as very healthy in the store. when we get home, we discover that the yogurt may not have any fat in it, but instead, it has a lot of sugar. it is no coincidence that many of us have been subjected to this. companies know that we want to make healthy choices, so they do everything possible to make food appear healthy, even though it is sometimes the exact opposite. a common trick is for the manufacturer to highlight a single ingredient that is perceived as useful and then to pump this out in large, bold letters while indicating, for example, the sugar and fat content in microscopic text. the agreement we are voting on tomorrow will put an end to this and many other forms of misleading advertising. better health claims must be scientifically substantiated and must not be used for products that are not overall good for health. in future, if companies want to highlight a particular ingredient that consumers perceive as useful in a food, this food should in principle not contain excessive levels of sugar, fat, salt or alcohol. of course, there is still a lot of work to be done on this regulation and we will be following closely the work ahead, especially the development of nutrition profiles. i and many in my group are very proud of this agreement, but we are also prepared to come back with further demands for tightening up if necessary.
for the verts/ale group. eels are the forerunners! long before we europeans began to cooperate across borders, the eels made it their way of life. they radiate into the sargasso sea from all over europe to meet and breed, regardless of national boundaries. but in the last 40 years, the eel population has declined by over 75 percent. the fastest decline has been in the last 20 years. however, eels do not reach sexual maturity for 20 years, and eels born today will not be available for fishing for many years. it also means that it will be many years before we see the results of the measures, so it is time to help all the countries. the deer has already been beaten more than it can take. given the eagles as a symbol of cross-border cooperation, it would be a symbolic failure for the whole of the eu and the future of the eu if we did not succeed in saving the eagle.
for the verts/ale group. varela suanzes-carpegna has done a very serious job, but we heard earlier tonight how bolivian president morales spoke about 500 years of colonial plunder. that time should be over. but then we have this illegal fishing agreement which is pure neo-colonialism. western sahara is occupied and no one recognizes the sovereignty of morocco. only if the amendments to exclude the occupied parts of the agreement are accepted can the european parliament's speech on human rights be implemented in practice. please read the sherpa legal analysis. they are experts in international agreements and condemn the fisheries agreement in its entirety. the legal services of the institutions have been used to misinterpret the un charter in the service of exploitation, but the legal services of parliament recognise that the agreement is only legal if it benefits the saharawi people. in the previous agreements, morocco completely ignored the population. there is nothing in this agreement about compensation to the saharawi people or targeted aid as compensation. morocco has stolen western sahara and whoever signs a contract with morocco for this fish is guilty of hypocrisy. it's just like asking a thief to go into someone's kitchen and steal their fish from the dining table, which no one in this room would do. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's fishing fleet is not disrupted by the introduction of new fishing vessels.
for the ind/dem group. we have previously pointed out how harmful eu fisheries agreements are, both for the people of the world's poorest countries and for eu taxpayers. the only beneficiaries are fisheries undertakings from mainly mediterranean countries whose undertakings are heavily subsidised by the agreements. this time, however, it is much more serious, because the eu is about to conclude an agreement with a colonial power of the worst kind. in this way, the eu indirectly approves the brutal and unlawful occupation of western sahara. it is extremely disturbing that the old european colonial powers continue to pursue the same policy as before, in subtler forms but with the same destructive effect. these fisheries agreements are highly reprehensible. they prevent poor countries from developing their own fishing industries, they contribute to overfishing of the world's oceans and they cost the eu taxpayers huge sums of money. i therefore urge you to vote against all fisheries agreements that come up for vote in parliament, but above all against this one.
there is much to be said about eu fisheries policy. there is a lot of politics in this, apart from the fisheries agreements themselves! this agreement contains a lot of politics and there are many ambiguities with regard to western sahara. morocco has occupied western sahara since 1975 and the international community has not yet been able to resolve this issue. morocco has refused to accept the un plan for a referendum and has unfortunately not contributed to any kind of dialogue or negotiation. i am very concerned that by voting for this report we are giving morocco the opportunity to exploit the fishery resources of western sahara and its people. the legal analyses are contradictory and several points to the fact that there is an unclear international legal situation as the boundaries of morocco's territorial waters are blurred. this could lead to morocco issuing fishing licences belonging to western sahara, which would be contrary both to the un charter and to international law. morocco has done it before and the money has not reached the people of western sahara. i will therefore support the amendments which limit the agreement to what is compatible with international law. i think it is important that the eu does not approve of the policy of occupation, but that we show that we want a solution to the conflict over this last colony in africa. if the amendments are not adopted, i intend, and many of us hope, to vote against this agreement. we cannot say that this is just about fishing and the conditions of fishermen. it is a matter of politics, and we must recognize that. we have a great responsibility to make our position known and we can do that by voting against this agreement.
this fisheries agreement is a shameful agreement which means that the eu is contributing to violations of international law! the agreement covers the fishing waters of western sahara, but morocco is the occupier and oppressor of western sahara and has no right to its natural resources. the legitimate representatives of western sahara, the polisario liberation movement, are firmly opposed to this agreement. the geneva convention prohibits an occupier from exploiting the natural resources of occupied territories for his own benefit, but that is precisely what this agreement entails. this means that the eu is also profiting from and legitimising the illegal occupation of western sahara by morocco and thus actively contributing to violations of international law. mr commissioner, you have negotiated an agreement with morocco which is a disgraceful agreement!
the pause is coming to an end, but it doesn't feel like much thought has been given to it around europe. the lively debate which was to be launched in all the member states, as all the prime ministers promised at the meeting on 18 june last year, has essentially been blighted by their absence. the presidency obviously does not think it is worth taking part in this debate either. it's not because people don't want to talk about europe. you know, i know, we all know that people want to discuss europe. in school classes, in workplaces, in debates, everywhere people have views, commitments and ideas about what we should do with our european cooperation, how it can be changed, how it can be improved and what it is that should not be included in it. what's missing is leadership. europe's leaders are too preoccupied with their own internal problems to raise their sights to the european level. but now it's time to stop the deprecation and instead show this leadership. if the state and foreign ministers don't want to take it, we will! of course, some institutional work is needed. some of your suggestions are very good. i believe that if we increase the openness and transparency of the council and introduce subsidiarity controls, which can be done without treaty amendment, that would be a step forward. we have high expectations of the finnish presidency. in this matter the light seems to come from the east. we need a constitution, of course, a set of rules that create legitimacy. i too believe that in the end there will be a revised constitution compared to the one we have now, but it will be built on the same basic ideas. we need to show action and commitment in foreign policy and law enforcement and stand up for enlargement. i hope that we can come up with concrete proposals in the near future. we all know that the pause will be extended. we must show that the eu is needed for jobs and the economy and to bring about continued reforms in the single market. we must show that europe is needed in the world and we must show the benefits of enlargement. a subject of much debate in europe, one of the topics on the commission's website, is institutional matters and the seat of the parliament. many people throughout europe think that the european parliament's two seats are a baroque idea. it costs a lot of money and puts a cloud over parliament. i hope that this citizens' initiative will soon land on margot wallström's table with a million signatures!
i would like to thank the committee for the work it has done and, of course, especially the rapporteur, mr rack. as many have said here, we need a more environmentally friendly transport system in europe. we have a very large environmental burden. at the same time, transport is very important for our regions and for businesses in the regions. combined transport will be very important in the future for the functioning of transport systems throughout the eu. at the same time, the marco polo project shows the different conditions under which we live in the different regions of the eu. in many regions, it is long distances. the companies there are often subcontractors to larger companies in more densely populated areas. they are very important for employment in the remote regions. therefore, the marco polo project must not lead to the relocation of employment in the union. i have acted on this view in committee and can now accept the compromise that exists with regard to article 1 of the report, which states that the marco polo project must create added value for the eu without adversely affecting economic, social or territorial cohesion, and the future article 2, which states that this must not have a negative impact on production or employment.
in writing. the commission is proposing to remove the customs duties on certain types of rice from india and pakistan, which we think is excellent. we moderates therefore vote in favour of the motion. at the same time, quotas are proposed to be maintained for a number of other rice varieties from the usa and thailand, in accordance with the agreements concluded by the commission with the respective countries. we believe that all duties on rice should be abolished, as they are harmful both to the exporting country, to consumers in the eu and to the general economic development.
in writing. we have voted against this report. we believe that trade in rice should in principle be free. this would benefit the household coffers of consumers.
in writing. the commission proposal differs from most proposals on fisheries agreements with third countries in that, instead of extending the agreement or introducing a new protocol, the eu terminates the current fisheries agreement with angola. the junilists consistently vote against the renewal of fisheries agreements and therefore welcome the termination of this agreement. we have therefore voted in favour of this report. the reason for terminating the agreement is that the commission has concerns about the angolan legislation on biological aquatic resources. the rapporteur regrets that the community and the angolan authorities have not been able to reach an agreement. we regret rather that the eu still intends to conclude new destructive fisheries agreements and to extend existing ones. the commission and the european parliament have not realised that fisheries agreements with third countries have a negative impact on the population of the countries concerned. we therefore have a very different approach to the rapporteur's approach to eu fisheries agreements with third countries.
in writing. we swedish social democrats would have liked to see parliament support the stronger proposals contained in both the commission's original proposal and the council's common position. however, it has not been possible to reach agreement on this content. we believe that the compromise reached is the best result we can achieve at the moment. the compromise will greatly strengthen consumer protection and make it easier for consumers to make healthy choices. that is why we supported the compromise and voted in accordance with it in all its parts.
in writing. voting declaration on the report on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on nutrition and health claims made on foods by poli bortone the commission's proposal to regulate nutrition and health claims is based on several assumptions which we doubt: firstly, that there is an absolute truth about what is healthy, secondly, that sales arguments and advertising are something negative that should be regulated, but also that the eating habits of citizens can be controlled by political decisions. finally, it is assumed that this is a political task for the eu. none of these conditions is valid. there is no absolute truth about what is harmful or useful, new facts are added and old truths are re-examined, which makes it directly inappropriate to force citizens into a certain behaviour through political decisions. political decisions cannot ensure that citizens eat all-round or determine the usefulness of individual products in their context. each individual must take responsibility for his diet according to his circumstances. products based on new findings must be given a chance to enter the market to compete with existing products. today there is no possibility of voting against the commission proposal, which is why we are voting in favour of the european parliament's proposal because it reduces the regulations compared to both the commission proposal and the council's position.
in writing. the european parliament voted today to conclude a fisheries agreement with morocco. we social democrats voted against the agreement. we believe that the fisheries agreement should not include the territory of western sahara, since morocco has occupied western sahara since 1975. we believe that the agreement is contrary to international law because there are no guarantees that it will benefit the people of western sahara, who were not involved in the drafting of the agreement.
in writing. the eu is to conclude a fisheries agreement with an african state. we have repeatedly pointed out the harmful nature of these agreements. the countries with which the eu concludes these agreements are deprived of the opportunity to develop their own well-functioning fishing industry. furthermore, the eu's fishing fleet contributes to overfishing, something that has been pointed out by both the swedish authorities and environmental organisations. in addition, these agreements are costly for the taxpayers of the eu member states. in fact, these are direct subsidies to the fishing industry. this agreement is particularly reprehensible, since morocco has been occupying western sahara illegally for many years. the international court of justice in the hague has pointed this out and this means that morocco has no sovereignty over natural resources, including fisheries resources. by concluding a fisheries agreement with morocco, the eu is indirectly approving this occupation and the human rights violations that continue to be committed by morocco in western sahara. we voted against the report in today's vote.
in writing. i'm abstaining from the vote. while there are positive aspects to the report, such as the need for the council to be more open, it focuses too much on growth and cost reduction when it should be emphasising sustainable development and cost-effectiveness. reduced costs are not an end in themselves, as they can lead to a continuous erosion of public budgets. cost-effectiveness is a better goal.
in writing. we have decided to abstain in the final vote on this 'deck-and-cover' resolution. our basic view is that the council of ministers should be the strongest political force in relation to the other eu institutions, since it represents the member states in an eu which we see as intergovernmental cooperation. this means that we also believe that the council of ministers, like the commission, should have the right to propose legislation. we also do not share the view expressed in paragraph b of the draft report. the commission is called upon to backtrack on the withdrawal of 68 legislative proposals which it considers not to be in line with the objectives of, inter alia, the principles of better regulation. we think it is good in principle that the commission is trying to clean up its vast catalogue of legislative proposals at eu level.
the question of bulgaria and romania's membership of the eu has already been de facto settled. the only thing we can discuss formally here is whether these two countries should be admitted on 1 january 2007 or 2008. the junilist welcomes the addition of new members for two reasons. firstly, the more we become, the more difficult it will be to imagine that the development of the european union can be regulated in detail from brussels. that's a big step forward. secondly, it is a great success for democracy and the rule of law and for peace in europe that we are bringing new members into the eu. in that sense, we are supporters of what is commonly called the soft power of the eu. the eu is indeed contributing to democracy and the rule of law in its neighbourhood for the simple reason that countries around the eu want to join the union for all sorts of reasons. and to join, we in the eu require these countries to meet important conditions, the copenhagen criteria. but then we have to take this seriously and it is obvious that bulgaria and romania do not meet the requirements we chose to set at the outset. for the same reason, we see that it was wrong to start membership negotiations with turkey now. the treatment of the kurds, women, freedom of speech and much else shows that we should not have done that. it was right, however, to reject negotiations with serbia for the time being because general mladic had not been found. what we can do now on the issue we are facing today is at least to postpone the accession of bulgaria and romania until january 2008. we will then be sending the right signal to europe and to croatia, serbia, turkey, ukraine and belarus for the future.
today's debate has been surrounded by a lot of pressure, speculation and, of course, a great deal of nervousness in the countries concerned. i am therefore pleased with the commission's message today that we hope to welcome our romanian and bulgarian friends into the community at the turn of the year. the road to that has been and is long and difficult. of course, it is not easy to overcome the consequences of the enormous oppression and misery caused by the communist dictatorships in romania and bulgaria. the politicians and people of these countries have made enormous efforts and sacrifices with a clear aim of reforming, democratizing and bringing their countries into the european community. there's still some work to be done. we are painfully aware of the problems that exist: corruption, organised crime, the difficulties in integrating the roma and the situation of orphans. we all know the criteria too, they are the same for everyone and we trust the commission to make an objective and professional assessment and evaluation. we also count on your wholehearted support and assistance to bulgaria and romania in overcoming these difficulties so that we can make progress towards membership by the end of the year. we in the european parliament will help in any way we can and we are convinced that this will go well in the time that remains. the liberal group has long advocated safety clauses for all of us, new and old members. we know that violations of the treaties and human rights are also taking place in the present eu and it would be good to find clearer mechanisms to deal with and draw attention to this. eu integration is like a tango, it takes two to make it work. the candidate countries must do their homework, but we must also do ours. we have internal problems in the eu that we have to deal with, as we discussed this morning with commissioner wallström. we must also have the courage to stand up for the importance of enlargement and explain to our citizens why it is so important that now romania and bulgaria come along with us and sit here and speak in the benches instead of sitting up there and just listening.
romania and bulgaria have been promised eu membership as of 1 january 2007. they have signed the accession treaty and we have sent signals to romania and bulgaria that they are doing a good job and that if they continue to work hard they will also become members according to the timetable. in the meantime, a number of things have happened in europe which have nothing to do with romania and bulgaria, but which have strongly influenced public opinion also on the question of the membership of these two countries. romania and bulgaria were promised membership before scepticism about further enlargement began to spread, before a number of european countries voted against the eu treaty proposal and before fear of social tourism began to spread in the old europe. the fact that these fears are unjustified and that today's political leaders have not been able to explain this and give people a sense of optimism is a clear sign that there is a serious lack of leadership in today's europe. but romania and bulgaria should not pay the price. i am both surprised and concerned that no one here today has mentioned that enlargement is really about security. several countries in the balkans are undergoing nation-state formation, which is a difficult and fragile process that can explode at any time and must be handled with both emotion and knowledge. in such a situation, it would not be sensible to postpone the accession of romania and bulgaria to the eu. not least because it would certainly draw both attention and energy away from much more complicated issues in the region such as the status of kosovo, the referendum in montenegro and the status of montenegro, and the constitutional changes currently under way in bosnia. romania and bulgaria have done a good job. hard work is needed and they will continue to work hard, just as other countries did in their first years as eu members. but for the security of europe, it would be best if the eu fulfilled its commitments to romania and bulgaria and instead concentrated its efforts on the other much more delicate issues that need to be resolved in the balkan region during the year.
one problem with emu is that there is no control by the financial markets over the way countries conduct their economic policies. however large the deficits in the debt of countries, their ratings are not affected significantly. there is an implicit guarantee for eurozone countries, regardless of what is said about the fact that their borrowing is not guaranteed by other eurozone countries. my view can be summed up in three points: new countries joining the eurozone must first have a referendum before they adopt the euro. it is important that the real meaning of currency exchange be made clear to citizens. they must realise the consequences of having a fixed exchange rate and not being able to conduct their own monetary policy. the example of sweden shows the importance of having a referendum. secondly, new countries with a public debt to gdp ratio exceeding 60% of gdp should not be accepted. this rule has been applied too laxly so far. thirdly, new countries should be forced to borrow part of their government borrowing in the long dollar market, for example in 10-year bonds. this would force a rating without a corresponding implicit guarantee from the eu countries. i think that such dollar bonds would give a more accurate picture of the economies of the countries
in writing. - today we voted in favour of the report by mr sergio sousa pinto on the interinstitutional agreement. the report considers that the interinstitutional agreement reached by the ep, the council and the commission is compatible with primary law and the treaties. it is positive that we have finally reached an agreement on the eu's long-term budget. at the same time, it is disappointing that no progress was made on budgetary reform. we would like to see a thorough review of the agreement on agricultural expenditure and a proper reform of regional aid. the eu's resources will be put to greater use in areas such as fighting crime, fighting poverty, combating climate change and research, as well as in making the eu the most competitive economy in the world. budget reform must be the focus of the agreed review to be carried out in 2008/2009. there are also parts of the report that we would have liked to have been without. the so-called globalisation fund and the 'own resources' paper are among them. we believe that the member states should continue to be the ones to decide on the resources available to the eu.
in writing. we have voted today in favour of the reimer böge report on the interinstitutional agreement on budgetary discipline. it is important that an agreement has finally been reached on the eu's long-term budget. we are, however, critical of the report's support for the so-called globalisation fund. globalisation brings enormous opportunities for the eu member states and, to the extent that it poses any challenges, they are best handled by the member states themselves. we also reject the report's suggestion that the eu should have its own resources instead of relying on contributions from the member states. the resources available to the union are decided by the member states. any form of eu tax would be a departure from that principle.
in writing. today we voted in favour of the reimer böge report on the interinstitutional agreement on budgetary discipline. the report approves the budgetary and financial implications of the agreement reached by the european parliament, the council and the commission. it is positive that we have finally reached an agreement on the eu's long-term budget. at the same time, it is disappointing that no progress was made on budgetary reform. we would like to see a thorough review of the agreement on agricultural expenditure and a proper reform of regional aid. the eu's resources will be put to greater use in areas such as fighting crime, fighting poverty, combating climate change and research, as well as in making the eu the most competitive economy in the world. budget reform must be the focus of the agreed review to be carried out in 2008/2009. there are also parts of the report that we would have liked to have been without. the so-called globalisation fund and the 'own resources' paper are among them. we believe that it should remain up to the member states to decide what resources the eu should have.
in writing. we have voted against both reports because we believe that the total payment appropriations should be within 1.00% of the eu countries' combined gnp, as proposed by the g6 countries. we also see no reason to celebrate the fact that the eu budget has been increased by eur 4 billion through another round of negotiations initiated by the federalist majority in the european parliament. that money would have been better used in the member states' political priorities for spending. we therefore reject the agreement on the 2007-13 budgetary plan. we also strongly oppose the discussions in this european parliament about reforming the own resources system and giving the eu its own source of revenue, for example by introducing a charge on mobile phones throughout the union. the eu budget is based on membership dues from the member states and the level of membership dues is decided by the council of ministers.
in writing. it is regrettable that the european parliament has not taken account of the g6's 1% target, but has instead worked towards a budgetary framework far in excess of this amount. the agreement also lacks ambition in terms of the necessary reforms of both agricultural and regional policy. within the framework of a long-term budget of 1% of gnp, there must be room to invest in scientific research and support enlargement. the review of the eu's financing system must under no circumstances lead to the eu being given its own taxation rights and it is therefore unfortunate that wording implies that this is necessary. i have therefore chosen to reject the financial implications of the interinstitutional agreement and to vote against the reports as a whole.
in writing. we swedish social democrats have chosen to support the compromise amendments to the dagmar roth-behrendt report on tses, which have been put forward jointly by the pes, epp-ed, alde, greens/efa and gue/ngl. we need a large majority in this parliament to support measures to combat and eradicate tses. we believe that the diet of ruminants should not be based on animal protein. however, experts in the field believe that this may be justified in an exceptional case. this concerns fishmeal as a protein substitute for calves. we believe that this process must be preceded by careful checks to ensure that the feed in question is fishmeal and not other animal protein.
in writing. it is good to extend the ban on animal feed to animals other than ruminants, but it is very unfortunate to make an exception allowing the feeding of fishmeal to calves. i therefore vote against this part of the proposal, but otherwise it is a good compromise.
in writing. technical standardisation measures are a prerequisite for the proper functioning of the internal market. it is therefore necessary to provide funding for the institutes responsible for developing common standards. however, the financing of the institutions and organisations concerned should be debated and decided within the framework of the ordinary budgetary negotiations. it is not responsible at this stage to determine the indicative amounts to be granted to the institutions concerned. we have therefore voted against this report.
in writing. this legislation is based on the fundamental fallacy that standardization is intended to increase the competitiveness of enterprises and not to facilitate consumers. it is also logical that industry should pay for standardization, even if only the firms benefit. i therefore oppose the eu's contribution to this work, which should be self-financed by levies. i therefore vote against the motion.
for the ind/dem group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. these are just a few brief comments. the single market is the most important source of prosperity that the eu gives us. the interconnection of gas and electricity networks, including in south-eastern europe, is also undoubtedly important for the internal market. it reduces, as we have heard from many speakers here, the risk of disruption in a production unit that knocks out large parts. it's going to be a stability. it is very good that we can also count on international regulatory authorities. perhaps, too, people in the region will learn to cooperate, so that the risk of violent conflicts of the kind we have seen before will be reduced. so far, very good. then comes the poison pill. suddenly it says that if you have a common market, then of course you also have a common foreign policy. it doesn't, but you can abuse it that way. what we are seeing here is an abuse of jean monnet's famous tradition. instead of openly discussing the pros and cons of a common foreign policy, it is being smuggled into a completely different context. it's a very common technique. we know that the people of europe do not want more supranationality. this is revealed as soon as the will of the people is released in referendums instead of being channeled through the political establishment. i am saying that the wording on a common foreign policy should be deleted. we are not going to push for it by means of solutions to energy problems in south-eastern europe.
for the ind/dem group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply. the committee on budgets report gives an elegant overview of its field, points to many issues of importance to developing countries, speaks of the importance of saving eu funds, and stresses the importance of strict priorities. is there anyone who objects to this? no, i'm not going. the problem with the report is this: it does not take a position on the level at which these important issues should be addressed. is it at eu or national level? it does not take a position on how the regulatory system should be changed to promote this better management and it does not take a position on what should be abolished or reduced in order to give priority to other things. let me make some suggestions. a very large part of what is proposed to stimulate growth to cope with the demands of globalisation, adaptation, etc., is something that the member states can best manage themselves in the noble competition with each other to find good solutions. this includes almost the whole of the lisbon agenda, small business policy and research activities. it is difficult to find any reason to regulate these issues at eu level. the important tasks at eu level are, for example, to put in order the management of carbon dioxide emission allowances. the eu has taken the lead in this area and it would be disastrous if this system, which russia and japan are to join in a few years' time, were to collapse in the meantime. the trading of emission allowances now needs to be restructured. an important step could be to allow member state governments to auction emission allowances to companies. in addition, allowances must be extended to cover larger sectors of the economy and more and more countries. this is a central task of a global nature for the european union. we talk about subsidiarity in passing, but when it becomes serious it disappears. the report says that almost every problem is something for the eu to tackle. the rules that need to be changed include those relating to agricultural policy. we must move towards the re-nationalisation of costs in order to eventually have the political means to liberalise the whole system. finally, we need to discuss concretely what needs to be eliminated or reduced when other priorities are to be given. we must seriously begin to analyse and discuss which ones are to be abolished by the eu institutions. we should, for example, close down the european economic and social committee and the committee of the regions. these are two institutions that have outlived themselves. we must finally get to the point where we make brussels the seat of the european parliament and let the building we are in now become the home of, say, an eu university. this would be extremely beneficial for parliament, but also for strasbourg as a city. this would mean that the city's service industries - hotels, restaurants, taxis, etc. - would be evenly loaded over time and year, instead of suffering from overload a few days a month and being dragged along with underemployment in between.
this report is very important because it is about growth reaching all our parts of europe. i support the report by mrs constance krehl and thank her for her excellent work and for the good cooperation. i would like to raise two points, and in particular to stress the importance of the social economy and the development of the information technology infrastructure. i would also like to emphasize the development of the information technology infrastructure. the social economy as a model for sustainable development, with its capacity to reconcile the economic and social fabric in the regions by coupling the spirit of entrepreneurship with social purposes, combating passive dependency on social welfare systems, needs to be recognised and taken into account in social and territorial cohesion policies and frameworks at european, national and local level. the reason is that a social economy brings entrepreneurship and creates job opportunities at local and regional level. a social economy targets socially excluded groups, for example the long-term unemployed. a social economy means innovative entrepreneurship for minority groups and it increases empowerment and strengthens civil society. i would also like to stress the importance of developing infrastructure for information technology. in the future, the commission will be able to take action to ensure that the eu's citizens are able to access the information they need to make their living. if new businesses are to be able to operate in all regions, there must be an information technology and infrastructure for them to enable them to operate effectively. the poorly developed information technology infrastructure must not be allowed to prevent action in the regions. i therefore think it is good that this is emphasized in the commission document and in mr krehl's report.
in writing. the promotion of human rights is an extremely important issue that should be taken into account in all policy areas. we believe that human rights are universal and that they should be fought for in all countries. the european parliament, however, is using this report to advance the eu's positions. we are opposed to several elements of the report, including the texts which seek to increase the power of the european parliament, and have therefore voted against the report. of course, we condemn all violations of human rights.
in writing. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the establishment of a european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products for human use (emah) is a step in the right direction. we are, however, critical of the report's assertion that the establishment of the so-called globalisation fund could be an important step forward for the eu. we also reject the report's suggestion that the eu should in the long term have its own resources rather than relying on contributions from the member states. we believe that it is up to the member states to decide what resources the union has at its disposal. any form of eu tax would be a departure from that principle.
in writing. the report proposes a farmers' insurance scheme co-financed by farmers, the member states and the eu under the common agricultural policy. we are not prepared to accept that. we swedish social democrats are opposed to new instruments and more funding for the agricultural sector. as the common agricultural policy (cap) is a major budgetary item, the sector should be able to finance itself support for farmers affected by natural disasters. furthermore, there is no room in the budget for increased appropriations. that is why we voted against the report.
in writing. in an eu of 25 member states, the report is too unilaterally focused on the problems of the european mediterranean. within the eu, there are a variety of problems arising from natural hazards, such as floods in central europe and storms and frosts in northern europe. nevertheless, forest fires in southern europe are constantly raised in the european parliament as a problem that must be tackled by the eu. but we believe that it is the member states concerned, and no one else, who have the responsibility for taking action in this area. on the contrary, if the responsibility for taking measures and creating preventive systems to prevent such disasters is taken away from the member states, the risks increase. in insurance theory, this is called "moral hazard". the report lists a wide range of measures at eu level with consequent pending expenditure, which will require significant budgetary increases. we oppose a specific eu programme for the protection of forests because we believe that this is a responsibility which lies at the level of the member states. the report's idea of eu-wide awareness campaigns to promote changing attitudes towards the use of fire (paragraph 23) is one of several examples where the ideological enrichment during the drafting of the report has completely lost touch with reality. we have therefore voted against the report.
in writing. in this report the rapporteur deals with natural disasters from an environmental point of view. the commission is proposing, among other things, the creation of a european observatory. we believe that transnational environmental problems should be dealt with at eu level. however, it is highly doubtful whether natural disasters are transnational. flooded rivers may be something to be solved at eu level, but there is already a river directive regulating how to tackle this type of problem. as far as fires are concerned, we believe that it is up to each member state to create the structures necessary to prevent them and their damaging effects. if the eu is to come in with money every time a natural disaster occurs, the incentive to work on prevention naturally diminishes. in the long run, this leads to more and more serious disasters, which is obviously very bad for the environment and costly for the taxpayers. the rapporteur calls for the eu to encourage member states to set up national forest funds for the prevention of natural disasters. we believe that this is a matter for the member states and that they should therefore decide for themselves whether it is necessary or not. for the reasons stated above, we voted against the report in today's vote.
in writing. the reports propose the creation of a number of funds and the increased use of the structural funds and the solidarity fund to finance eu measures to prevent and combat natural disasters. the proposals include the creation of a european monitoring centre for drought and desertification, a european fire and forestry fund and a disaster fund under the common agricultural policy, as well as intensive information campaigns on sustainable water consumption. it is also proposed to extend the european solidarity fund to cover the consequences of public health crises, terrorist acts and technological disasters. the junilist welcomes international solidarity when a country is hit by disasters. however, the eu should not use these disasters as an argument to expand its power and develop a common policy in this area or to set up additional institutions. if the responsibility for taking measures and creating systems to prevent such disasters is shifted from the member states, the risks increase. in insurance theory, this is called "moral hazard". we have therefore voted against both reports.

mr president, i would like to thank you. this debate reminds me a little of a famous tv sketch by the british comedians of monthy pyton. in this sketch, a man returns to the pet store to advertise a dead parrot. although the parrot is obviously dead, the salesman refuses to accept this and instead comes up with a series of strange excuses. the parrot can be compared to the eu constitution, and the salesman can be compared to the eu political elite. it's time to realize that the parrot is really dead. only then will the debate on the future of europe be meaningful again. in fact, the voters in the netherlands and france gave us a clear and unequivocal message. they want more democracy and less central government. they want less free market forces and more social responsibility. that is the seed of better cooperation in europe, not the constitution.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the national state is the carrier of democracy in europe. democracy as a political system presupposes a demos, a people whose citizens feel a strong sense of community with each other and who are therefore prepared to take on far-reaching responsibilities for each other. a demos has common cultural traditions and common history. a demos has a common political arena, where debate can take place and where conflicts can be clarified and resolved in democratic forms. a demos has a national identity that creates the soil in which democracy can grow and in which it can stand robust and survive the strain. guy verhofstadt is a typical representative of the euro-elite who believe that national identities can be replaced by a fast-made european identity in which flags, anthems and national days can be sold quickly, almost like new brands. by attempting to push forward a political union that lacks legitimacy in the eyes of europeans, the eu establishment now risks creating popular opposition to the whole integration project. the freedom for us europeans to trade with each other, to settle where we want, to study where we want and to invest where we want is what verhofstadt disdainfully calls 'just a free trade zone'. freedom is not just freedom, but freedom is the best thing that the world can seek. so goes an old medieval swedish folk song. i would remind you that if we try to impose on the countries, by means of a constitution, a policy which will always lack popular support, we are setting the stage for the collapse of the european union.
acting president of the council . the commission has already made a number of proposals. there is probably no other partner in the world with which the european union has such close and intense relations as with the united states of america. a well-functioning and sustainable network of contacts is therefore of course also an important and central concern for any presidency. looking back, 2005 was marked by positive developments in relations overall. we have made considerable progress on a number of issues and we are engaged in a dialogue, often in the run-up to policy decisions, to bring views closer together. the european union has a duty to help the countries of central and eastern europe to develop their economic and social cohesion. we have repeatedly spoken together in after the elections - which were not free and fair - to impose travel restrictions and targeted financial sanctions against government members and other leaders. the same applies to the promotion of democracy in ukraine, where we are working closely with the united states to strengthen democratic and market economy structures. another example of a very close and successful continuation of contact group . we are committed to a continued international presence and we are convinced that close, equal cooperation between the eu and nato in the field of security policy is essential. other
